# PART 10 — PHASE-WISE ANTIGRAVITY MASTER PROMPTS

## Global instructions for every phase

Antigravity must:

1. Inspect the existing repository before editing.
2. Preserve stable functionality.
3. Never rewrite working modules without justification.
4. Never invent environment variables or credentials.
5. Use TypeScript strictly.
6. Validate all input with Zod.
7. Enforce server-side authorization.
8. Never expose private user information.
9. Never make financial data client-writable.
10. Write tests for every critical module.
11. Keep a changelog.
12. Run production build after changes.
13. Report completed and incomplete tasks separately.
14. Never claim a test passed unless it actually ran.
15. Stop and report if a required external account, legal approval or credential is missing.

---

## PHASE 0 — Repository Audit & Foundation

**Goal:** Understand the repository and establish a safe development foundation.

```text
You are the Lead Software Architect and Senior
Full-Stack Engineer for JANOSHOKTI.

Your first task is NOT to start coding.

Inspect the complete repository.

Determine:
- Existing Next.js version.
- TypeScript configuration.
- Firebase client configuration.
- Firebase Admin configuration.
- Existing authentication implementation.
- Firestore collections and rules.
- Existing API routes.
- Existing environment variables.
- Deployment configuration.
- Existing dependencies.
- Existing security controls.

Create:

1. ARCHITECTURE.md
2. SRS.md
3. DATABASE_SCHEMA.md
4. API_CONTRACTS.md
5. SECURITY_MODEL.md
6. RBAC_MATRIX.md
7. IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md
8. ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLES.md
9. RISK_REGISTER.md

Requirements:

- Do not expose secrets.
- Do not modify production functionality.
- Identify security vulnerabilities.
- Identify conflicting dependencies.
- Identify missing tests.
- Identify database migration risks.

Deliver a detailed audit report before making
any architectural changes.

Wait for approval before proceeding.
```

## PHASE 1 — Authentication & Core Foundation

**Goal:** Google authentication, user profile and private data separation.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 1.

Requirements:

1. Firebase Google Authentication.
2. No separate registration form.
3. Automatic user provisioning.
4. Secure user profile.
5. Private profile collection.
6. Profile update.
7. Account suspension support.
8. Account deletion request workflow.
9. Centralized Firebase Admin authentication.
10. Server-side RBAC foundation.

Build:

- Login page.
- Public homepage shell.
- User dashboard.
- Profile page.
- Admin login protection.
- API authentication middleware.

Security:

- Never trust client-provided UID.
- Verify Firebase ID tokens on server.
- Reject suspended users.
- Keep phone numbers private.
- Protect private Firestore documents.
- Never expose Firebase Admin credentials.

Tests:

- Valid login.
- Invalid token.
- Expired token.
- Suspended account.
- Cross-user access.
- Unauthorized admin access.

Run lint, typecheck, tests and production build.

Report all changes.
```

## PHASE 2 — Idea Submission & AI

**Goal:** Complete idea collection and processing.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 2.

Create:

- Idea submission form.
- Idea management dashboard.
- Private submitter information.
- Submission receipt.
- Idea status machine.
- File attachment support.
- Consent management.
- AI processing queue.

AI modules:

1. Language detection.
2. Category classification.
3. Duplicate detection.
4. Summary generation.
5. Feasibility analysis.
6. Budget anomaly detection.
7. Risk identification.
8. Content moderation.

Use a provider-independent AI adapter.

AI must never make final approval decisions.

Requirements:

- Validate all input with Zod.
- Add rate limits.
- Add retry limits.
- Cache unchanged AI results.
- Track AI cost.
- Keep AI logs separate from public content.
- Provide manual fallback.

Create test fixtures for Bangla and English ideas.

Test private data isolation.

Complete the phase only when all tests pass.
```

## PHASE 3 — Public Notice Board & Community

**Goal:** Public idea publication and evaluation.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 3.

Build:

- Public Notice Board.
- Category filtering.
- Location filtering.
- Search.
- Pagination.
- Idea detail pages.
- Anonymous public presentation.
- Five-star rating.
- Constructive comments.
- Positive/negative feedback.
- Report abuse system.
- Moderation dashboard.

Publication:

- Monthly review cycle.
- Last ten days as default publication period.
- Approved ideas only.
- Publication consent required.
- Preserve historical records.

Security:

- No submitter name in public DTO.
- No submitter phone in public DTO.
- No private profile access.
- One active rating per user.
- Prevent self-rating.
- Protect against vote manipulation.

Create automated tests for every permission.
```

## PHASE 4 — Funding & Financial Ledger

**Goal:** Safe donation and accounting infrastructure.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 4.

This is a financial-critical phase.

Do not activate live payments automatically.

First implement a payment sandbox.

Build:

1. General funding campaigns.
2. Project-specific campaigns.
3. Optional donations.
4. Checkout API.
5. Provider adapter.
6. Webhook verification.
7. Idempotency.
8. Donation receipts.
9. Refund records.
10. Double-entry ledger.
11. Reconciliation.
12. Public transparency dashboard.

Rules:

- Store money as integer poisha.
- Never trust browser payment success.
- Never trust client-supplied totals.
- Never modify posted ledger entries.
- Use reversal entries.
- Preserve historical cycles.
- Separate general and restricted project funds.
- Mask donor personal information.
- Enforce dual authorization for high-risk actions.

Require documented legal and merchant approval
before production payment activation.

Test:
- Duplicate webhook.
- Forged webhook.
- Wrong amount.
- Failed payment.
- Refund.
- Partial settlement.
- Ledger imbalance.
- Unauthorized expense approval.

Do not release this phase if any financial
integrity test fails.
```

## PHASE 5 — Project & Transparency Management

**Goal:** Track real project execution.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 5.

Build:

- Project approval workflow.
- Project manager assignment.
- Project budgets.
- Milestones.
- Progress reporting.
- Expense requests.
- Vendor records.
- Expense approval.
- Completion reports.
- Project cancellation.
- Refund and unspent fund handling.
- Impact dashboard.

Every project must have:
- Project ID.
- Approved budget.
- Funding source.
- Responsible manager.
- Timeline.
- Milestones.
- Spending history.
- Public status.

Keep project funding separate from
organization operating funds.

Never allow project managers to approve
their own financial expense requests.

Generate public project reports from
verified records.
```

## PHASE 6 — Governance, HR & Employee Welfare

**Goal:** Add organization-wide management.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 6.

Build governance and HR modules:

- Board management.
- Role assignment.
- Approval workflows.
- Conflict of interest declarations.
- Audit management.
- Employee records.
- Employment contracts.
- Payroll.
- Leave records.
- Welfare accounting.
- Applicable retirement benefits.

Requirements:

- Restrict HR access.
- Restrict salary information.
- Keep employee welfare records separate
  from donation ledgers.
- Enforce segregation of duties.
- Preserve historical changes.
- Add approval history.
- Do not promise unapproved pension benefits.
- Follow applicable Bangladesh employment law.

All sensitive HR APIs must be server-side.
```

## PHASE 7 — Production Hardening

```text
Perform a complete production-readiness audit
for JANOSHOKTI.

Inspect:

- Authentication.
- Authorization.
- Firestore Rules.
- API security.
- Payment security.
- Financial accounting.
- Privacy.
- AI abuse.
- Rate limiting.
- Data retention.
- Backup and restoration.
- Performance.
- Accessibility.
- Mobile responsiveness.
- Monitoring.
- Incident response.

Run:

1. TypeScript checks.
2. ESLint.
3. Unit tests.
4. Integration tests.
5. E2E tests.
6. Firestore Rules Emulator tests.
7. Dependency audit.
8. Production build.
9. Security test suite.

Do not claim production readiness based
on build success alone.

Produce:

- FINAL_AUDIT.md
- SECURITY_REPORT.md
- PERFORMANCE_REPORT.md
- RELEASE_READINESS.md
- REMAINING_RISKS.md

Do not deploy or activate live fundraising
without explicit human approval.
```

It must satisfy all of these:

- [ ] No separate registration form.

- [ ] Legal and operational launch requirements are met.

<WritingBlock id="73184" variant="document"># JANOSHOKTI
## Software Requirements Specification (SRS) & Technical Architecture
**Version:** 1.0  
**Project Status:** Planning / Engineering Specification  
**Initial Region:** Rangpur, Bangladesh  
**Platform:** Web-first, Android-ready  
**Primary Language:** Bangla and English  
**Architecture:** Next.js + Firebase + AI-assisted workflows

---

# PART 1 — SOFTWARE REQUIREMENTS SPECIFICATION

## 1.1 Project Overview

JANOSHOKTI is a public social innovation platform where individuals can submit ideas to solve social, economic, educational, agricultural, technological and environmental problems.

The platform will:

- Collect public ideas without submission fees.
- Classify and analyze ideas using AI.
- Organize ideas for human expert review.
- Publish approved ideas anonymously.
- Allow public ratings and constructive criticism.
- Collect voluntary donations through authorized payment providers.
- Track project implementation and expenditure.
- Publish transparent financial and impact reports.
- Eventually create sustainable employment and employee welfare programs.

### Core Principle

Public donations must never be treated as personal income, investment capital belonging to a donor, or a payment for employment opportunities.

## 1.2 Business Objectives

| ID | Objective |
|---|---|
| BO-01 | Make idea submission accessible to everyone |
| BO-02 | Improve idea quality through AI and human review |
| BO-03 | Encourage public participation |
| BO-04 | Create transparent community funding |
| BO-05 | Convert approved ideas into real projects |
| BO-06 | Publish verifiable financial records |
| BO-07 | Measure social impact |
| BO-08 | Create sustainable employment opportunities |
| BO-09 | Establish long-term employee welfare policies |

## 1.3 User Types

1. Anonymous Visitor
2. Google-authenticated User
3. Idea Submitter
4. Donor
5. Expert Reviewer
6. Idea Moderator
7. Project Manager
8. Finance Officer
9. Auditor
10. Governance Administrator
11. Super Administrator

A single Google account may have multiple capabilities, but permissions must be explicitly assigned.

## 1.4 Functional Requirements

### FR-001: Authentication

- Google OAuth through Firebase Authentication.
- No separate password registration form.
- First successful login creates a user record.
- Server verifies Firebase ID tokens.
- User account suspension must be supported.
- Sign-out and account deletion request must be supported.
- Sensitive profile data must not be included in public responses.

### FR-002: Idea Submission

Required fields:

| Field | Type | Required |
|---|---|---|
| Full name | String | Yes |
| Phone | String | Yes |
| Idea title | String | Yes |
| Problem statement | Text | Yes |
| Detailed solution | Text | Yes |
| Category | Reference | Suggested |
| Expected beneficiaries | Text | Yes |
| Estimated budget | Number | Optional |
| Implementation district | String | Yes |
| Implementation upazila | String | Optional |
| Submitter district | String | Yes |
| Submitter upazila | String | Optional |
| Supporting files | Array | Optional |
| Consent version | String | Yes |

Submission rules:

- Google authentication is mandatory.
- Duplicate submissions must be detected.
- Rate limiting is mandatory.
- A submission receipt must be generated.
- Submission date must be server-generated.
- Private information must be stored separately.
- Submitter identity must never be exposed in public API responses.

### FR-003: Idea Management

Supported statuses:

- DRAFT
- SUBMITTED
- AI_PROCESSING
- AI_COMPLETED
- UNDER_REVIEW
- REVISION_REQUESTED
- APPROVED
- SCHEDULED
- PUBLISHED
- REJECTED
- ARCHIVED
- WITHDRAWN

Every status transition must be recorded.

### FR-004: AI Processing

AI responsibilities:

1. Language detection.
2. Category classification.
3. Idea summarization.
4. Similarity detection.
5. Duplicate identification.
6. Feasibility assessment.
7. Budget anomaly detection.
8. Risk identification.
9. Content moderation.
10. Reviewer assistance.

AI must not independently:

- Approve projects.
- Reject ideas permanently.
- Transfer funds.
- Select employees.
- Decide donor eligibility.

### FR-005: Public Notice Board

- Monthly publication cycle.
- Last ten days of each month as the default publication period.
- Approved ideas only.
- Anonymous publication.
- Category filters.
- District and upazila filters.
- Search.
- Sorting.
- Pagination.
- Five-star ratings.
- Constructive comments.
- Positive and negative feedback.
- Report abuse functionality.

A published idea must retain its publication history even if later archived.

### FR-006: Rating System

- One active rating per authenticated user per idea.
- Rating range: 1–5.
- Users can update their rating.
- Users cannot rate their own idea.
- Suspicious rating patterns must be flagged.
- Public results must show average rating and rating count.
- Rating data must not be the sole basis for project approval.

### FR-007: Comment System

- Authenticated commenting.
- Maximum comment length configured by admin.
- Spam protection.
- Report functionality.
- Moderation queue.
- Comment edit history.
- No public exposure of private account information.

### FR-008: Fundraising

Two fund types:

**A. General Fund**

For clearly disclosed organization-wide social activities.

**B. Project Fund**

For a specific approved project.

Donation requirements:

- Optional amount.
- Donor privacy preference.
- Payment provider.
- Unique internal receipt ID.
- Provider transaction reference.
- Payment verification.
- Refund support.
- Reconciliation.
- Donation history.

A donation must never be marked successful merely because the browser redirects to a success page.

### FR-009: Financial Transparency

Public dashboard:

- Total verified donations.
- Project-wise donations.
- Approved budgets.
- Verified expenses.
- Remaining balances.
- Refunds.
- Funding targets.
- Project financial reports.
- Audit reports.

Donor phone numbers must be masked. Full payment credentials and original sensitive transaction identifiers must remain private.

### FR-010: Project Management

- Project creation.
- Project approval.
- Project assignment.
- Budget approval.
- Milestone management.
- Expense requests.
- Expense verification.
- Vendor management.
- Progress reports.
- Completion reports.
- Cancellation and refund procedures.

### FR-011: Employee Management

Future module:

- Employee profiles.
- Employment contracts.
- Salary structures.
- Payroll.
- Leave management.
- Service history.
- Applicable statutory benefits.
- Approved provident fund or pension administration.
- Retirement records.

Employment benefits must follow applicable Bangladesh law and approved organizational policies.

### FR-012: Administration

- Role-based permissions.
- User management.
- Idea moderation.
- Project management.
- Financial administration.
- AI configuration.
- Monthly cycle management.
- Audit logs.
- Website settings.
- Security monitoring.

---

# PART 2 — SYSTEM ARCHITECTURE

## 2.1 High-Level Architecture

```text
                    PUBLIC USERS
                         |
                         v
                NEXT.JS WEB APPLICATION
                         |
                FIREBASE AUTHENTICATION
                         |
                         v
                SECURE API LAYER
                         |
          +--------------+--------------+
          |              |              |
          v              v              v
      IDEA SERVICE    AI SERVICE    PAYMENT SERVICE
          |              |              |
          v              v              v
      FIRESTORE       AI PROVIDER    PAYMENT PROVIDER
          |              |              |
          +--------------+--------------+
                         |
                         v
                 INTERNAL REVIEW
                         |
                         v
                PROJECT MANAGEMENT
                         |
                         v
              FINANCIAL LEDGER SYSTEM
                         |
                         v
              PUBLIC TRANSPARENCY
```

## 2.2 Technology Stack

| Component | Technology |
|---|---|
| Frontend | Next.js App Router |
| Language | TypeScript |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS |
| UI | Accessible component system |
| Authentication | Firebase Auth |
| Database | Cloud Firestore |
| Backend | Next.js Route Handlers |
| Admin SDK | Firebase Admin |
| AI | Provider-independent AI adapter |
| Queue | Cloud Tasks or equivalent |
| Cache | Upstash Redis |
| File Storage | Firebase Storage |
| Hosting | Vercel |
| CDN / Protection | Cloudflare |
| Monitoring | Sentry |
| Analytics | Privacy-conscious analytics |
| Testing | Vitest + Playwright |
| Validation | Zod |

## 2.3 Architecture Rules

1. All sensitive operations must execute server-side.
2. Client applications must never access financial admin operations directly.
3. Firestore Admin SDK access must be protected by explicit server authorization.
4. AI processing must run asynchronously.
5. Financial records must be immutable.
6. Public APIs must return sanitized DTOs.
7. All administrative actions must be auditable.
8. Future Android clients must use the same authenticated API contracts.
9. All money values must use integer BDT poisha, not floating-point numbers.
10. All timestamps must be stored in UTC and displayed in Bangladesh time.

```json
  "debitPoisha": 10000,
  "creditPoisha": 0,
  "createdAt": "SERVER_TIMESTAMP"
}
```

Second entry:

```json
{
  "transactionId": "TXN-001",
  "accountCode": "PROJECT_RESTRICTED_FUND",
  "debitPoisha": 0,
  "creditPoisha": 10000,
  "createdAt": "SERVER_TIMESTAMP"
}
```

Every posted transaction must satisfy:

**Total Debits = Total Credits**

Corrections must use reversal transactions, never destructive edits.

# PART 4 — API CONTRACTS

Base URL:

```text
/api/v1
```

All authenticated requests use:

```http
Authorization: Bearer FIREBASE_ID_TOKEN
Content-Type: application/json
```

All APIs must return a consistent response structure.

Success:

```json
{
  "success": true,
  "data": {},
  "requestId": "REQ-001"
}
```

Error:

```json
{
  "success": false,
  "error": {
    "code": "VALIDATION_ERROR",
    "message": "Invalid request",
    "fields": {}
  },
  "requestId": "REQ-001"
}
```

## 4.1 Authentication

| Method | Endpoint | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| GET | `/auth/me` | Current user |
| POST | `/auth/account-deletion-request` | Request account deletion |

Firebase handles Google authentication. Do not create a second custom password authentication system.

## 4.2 Ideas

| Method | Endpoint | Permission |
|---|---|---|
| POST | `/ideas` | Authenticated |
| GET | `/ideas` | Public |
| GET | `/ideas/:id` | Public if published |
| GET | `/ideas/mine` | Authenticated |
| PATCH | `/ideas/:id` | Owner, eligible statuses |
| POST | `/ideas/:id/withdraw` | Owner |
| POST | `/ideas/:id/report` | Authenticated |

Submission request:

```json
{
  "title": "Community Agriculture Hub",
  "problemStatement": "Problem description",
  "solutionDescription": "Solution details",
  "expectedBeneficiaries": "Local farmers",

  },
```

Server must validate the Google UID from the verified token. Never accept a client-supplied UID as identity proof.

# PART 5 — FIRESTORE SECURITY RULES

## 5.1 Security Policy

- Default deny.
- Users can only read their own private data.
- Public users can only read published public ideas.
- Users cannot directly write financial records.
- Users cannot assign themselves admin roles.
- AI results are server-written.
- Admin roles are server-controlled.
- Audit logs are server-written.
- Financial ledger is server-written.
- Payment webhooks are server-only.
- Sensitive profile data is never public.

## 5.2 Initial Firestore Rules

The following is a baseline for the initial collections. It must be tested against the final schema before production deployment.

```javascript
rules_version = '2';

service cloud.firestore {
  match /databases/{database}/documents {

    function signedIn() {
      return request.auth != null;
    }

    function isOwner(uid) {
      return signedIn() &&
             request.auth.uid == uid;
    }

    function activeUser() {
      return signedIn() &&
        exists(/databases/$(database)/documents/users/$(request.auth.uid)) &&
        get(/databases/$(database)/documents/users/$(request.auth.uid))
          .data.accountStatus == "ACTIVE";
    }

    function hasRole(role) {
      return activeUser() &&
        exists(/databases/$(database)/documents/admin_roles/$(request.auth.uid)) &&
        get(/databases/$(database)/documents/admin_roles/$(request.auth.uid))
          .data.roles.hasAny([role]);
    }

    function isReviewer() {
      return hasRole("IDEA_REVIEWER") ||
             hasRole("GOVERNANCE_ADMIN") ||
             hasRole("SUPER_ADMIN");
    }

    match /users/{uid} {
      allow read: if isOwner(uid);
      allow create: if isOwner(uid)
        && request.resource.data.keys().hasOnly([
          "uid",
          "displayName",
          "photoURL",
          "emailVerified",
          "accountStatus",
          "createdAt",
          "updatedAt",
          "lastLoginAt"
        ])
        && request.resource.data.uid == uid
        && request.resource.data.accountStatus == "ACTIVE";

      allow update: if isOwner(uid)
        && request.resource.data.diff(resource.data)
          .affectedKeys().hasOnly([
            "displayName",
            "photoURL",
            "updatedAt",
            "lastLoginAt"
          ]);

      allow delete: if false;
    }

    match /private_profiles/{uid} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /admin_roles/{uid} {
      allow read: if isOwner(uid) || hasRole("SUPER_ADMIN");
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /ideas/{ideaId} {
      allow read: if resource.data.status == "PUBLISHED"
                  && resource.data.visibility == "PUBLIC"
                  || (
                    activeUser() &&
                    resource.data.submitterUid == request.auth.uid
                  )
                  || isReviewer();

      allow create, update, delete: if false;
    }

    match /idea_reviews/{reviewId} {
      allow read: if isReviewer();
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /idea_votes/{voteId} {
      allow read: if true;
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /idea_comments/{commentId} {
      allow read: if resource.data.status == "PUBLISHED";
      allow create, update, delete: if false;
    }

    match /categories/{categoryId} {
      allow read: if true;
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /projects/{projectId} {
      allow read: if resource.data.status != "DRAFT";
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /project_milestones/{milestoneId} {
      allow read: if resource.data.visibility == "PUBLIC";
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /donations/{donationId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /payment_events/{eventId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /ledger_transactions/{transactionId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /ledger_entries/{entryId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /expenses/{expenseId} {
      allow read: if isReviewer() ||
        hasRole("FINANCE_OFFICER") ||
        hasRole("AUDITOR");
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /employees/{employeeId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /payroll_records/{payrollId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /welfare_records/{recordId} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }

    match /audit_logs/{logId} {
      allow read: if hasRole("AUDITOR") ||
                  hasRole("SUPER_ADMIN");
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /system_settings/{settingId} {
      allow read: if false;
      allow write: if false;
    }

    match /{document=**} {
      allow read, write: if false;
    }
  }
}
```

## 5.3 Critical Security Notes

1. Firebase Admin SDK bypasses Firestore Security Rules. Therefore, every privileged server operation must independently check authorization.
2. `hasRole()` above is only a baseline role lookup. Production code must validate role status, scope, expiry and account suspension.
3. Public comments and ratings should be written through controlled server endpoints.
4. Firestore queries must be compatible with security rules. Rules do not act as automatic filters.
5. Private profile data should use a separate server-only collection.
6. Financial collections must never become client-writable.
7. Firestore Rules Emulator tests are mandatory.
8. App Check and API rate limiting should be enabled.

---

# PART 6 — ADMIN PERMISSION MATRIX

| Permission | Super Admin | Governance | Reviewer | Finance | Project Manager | Auditor |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| View dashboard | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| Manage users | Yes | Limited | No | No | No | Read |
| View private idea identity | Restricted | Approved cases | Assigned cases | No | No | Authorized audit |
| Review ideas | Yes | Yes | Yes | No | No | Read |
| Approve ideas | Yes* | Yes* | No | No | No | No |
| Publish ideas | Yes | Yes | No | No | No | No |
| Create projects | Yes | Yes | No | No | Assigned | No |
| Approve project budgets | Two-person approval | Yes* | No | Yes* | Request | Read |
| Verify payments | No unilateral action | No | No | Yes | No | Read |
| Approve expenses | No unilateral action | No | No | Yes* | Request | Read |
| View ledger | Read | Read | No | Yes | Assigned project | Yes |
| Delete financial history | Never | Never | Never | Never | Never | Never |
| Manage roles | Yes* | No | No | No | No | No |
| View audit logs | Yes | Yes | Limited | Financial scope | Project scope | Yes |

`*` = Subject to segregation of duties and dual approval where applicable.

## Permission Implementation

Use explicit permission strings rather than relying only on role names.

Example:

```typescript
const PERMISSIONS = {
  IDEA_READ_PRIVATE: "idea:read_private",
  IDEA_REVIEW: "idea:review",
  IDEA_APPROVE: "idea:approve",
  IDEA_PUBLISH: "idea:publish",

  PROJECT_CREATE: "project:create",
  PROJECT_APPROVE: "project:approve",
  PROJECT_MANAGE: "project:manage",

  DONATION_VERIFY: "donation:verify",
  EXPENSE_REQUEST: "expense:request",
  EXPENSE_APPROVE: "expense:approve",
  LEDGER_READ: "ledger:read",

  USER_MANAGE: "user:manage",
  ROLE_MANAGE: "role:manage",
  AUDIT_READ: "audit:read"
} as const;
```

Every API should call a centralized authorization service.

# PART 7 — AI PROCESSING ARCHITECTURE

## 7.1 Processing Pipeline

```text
IDEA SUBMITTED
      |
      v
INPUT VALIDATION
      |
      v
LANGUAGE DETECTION
      |
      v
CONTENT MODERATION
      |
      v
CATEGORY CLASSIFICATION
      |
      v
DUPLICATE DETECTION
      |
      v
SUMMARY GENERATION
      |
      v
FEASIBILITY ANALYSIS
      |
      v
BUDGET RISK ANALYSIS
      |
      v
AI REPORT GENERATED
      |
      v
HUMAN REVIEW QUEUE
```

## 7.2 AI Output Schema

```json
{
  "ideaId": "IDEA-2026-000001",
  "language": "bn",
  "category": "agriculture",
  "subcategory": "storage",
  "summary": "Short summary",
  "problemClarityScore": 80,
  "feasibilityScore": 75,
  "socialImpactScore": 90,
  "budgetRisk": "MEDIUM",
  "riskFlags": [],
  "duplicateCandidates": [],
  "missingInformation": [],
  "recommendation": "HUMAN_REVIEW_REQUIRED",
  "confidence": 0.91,
  "modelVersion": "configured-model-version"
}
```

These scores are advisory. Do not represent them as scientifically validated probability estimates.

## 7.3 Cost Optimization

- Rule-based validation before AI.
- Embedding-based similarity before expensive analysis.
- Cache unchanged results.
- Use smaller models for classification.
- Use advanced models only when needed.
- Background processing.
- Retry failed jobs with limits.
- Track token usage.
- Monthly spending budget.
- AI kill switch.
- Manual review fallback.

---

# PART 8 — FINANCIAL AND PAYMENT ARCHITECTURE

## 8.1 Double-entry accounting

Each financial event must create balanced entries.

Examples:

- Donation received.
- Gateway fee.
- Bank settlement.
- Expense payment.
- Refund.
- Correction.
- Fund transfer between approved accounting categories.

Never overwrite posted transactions.

## 8.2 Payment Flow

```text
DONOR
  |
  v
CHECKOUT REQUEST
  |
  v
SERVER VALIDATES CAMPAIGN
  |
  v
PAYMENT PROVIDER SESSION
  |
  v
DONOR COMPLETES PAYMENT
  |
  v
AUTHENTICATED WEBHOOK
  |
  v
PROVIDER VERIFICATION
  |
  v
IDEMPOTENCY CHECK
  |
  v
ATOMIC LEDGER POSTING
  |
  v
RECEIPT GENERATION
  |
  v
PUBLIC TOTAL UPDATE
```

## 8.3 Mandatory Controls

- Idempotency keys.
- Provider webhook signature verification.
- Server-side payment verification.
- Reconciliation reports.
- Refund tracking.
- Dual approval for specified financial operations.
- Immutable transaction history.
- Daily backups.
- Independent accounting review.
- Restricted bank access.

Do not activate public payment collection until the organization's legal structure, applicable fundraising permissions, merchant onboarding and banking arrangements have been reviewed and approved.

## 8.4 Reset Policy

A funding cycle can be closed or replaced, but:

- Donation history cannot be deleted.
- Financial totals cannot be manually zeroed.
- Existing campaigns retain their historical data.
- Reconciliation must finish before closure.
- Every cycle closure must have an audit record.

---

# PART 9 — TESTING, DEPLOYMENT AND OPERATIONS

## 9.1 Required Testing

### Unit Tests
- Validation.
- Permissions.
- Financial calculations.
- Status transitions.
- AI output parsing.

### Integration Tests
- Google authentication.
- Firestore operations.
- Payment webhook.
- Ledger posting.
- Project approvals.
- Audit logs.

### Security Tests
- Unauthorized role escalation.
- IDOR.
- Private profile leakage.
- Cross-user data access.
- Duplicate payment webhook.
- Amount manipulation.
- Vote manipulation.
- Admin API abuse.
- Rate-limit bypass.

### End-to-End Tests
- Sign in.
- Submit idea.
- AI processing.
- Human review.
- Publication.
- Rating.
- Comment.
- Donation simulation.
- Financial report.

Use sandbox payment credentials for payment testing.

## 9.2 Production Release Gates

The release cannot be approved unless:

- TypeScript passes.
- Lint passes.
- Production build passes.
- Firestore Rules tests pass.
- API authorization tests pass.
- Payment sandbox tests pass.
- Privacy tests pass.
- Backup restoration is verified.
- Audit logging is verified.
- No unresolved critical or high security vulnerability remains.
- Legal and operational launch approvals are recorded.

## 9.3 Deployment Environments

```text
Development
    |
    v
Testing / Staging
    |
    v
Production
```

Separate Firebase projects, payment credentials, environment variables and storage buckets must be used for each environment.

Example environment variables:

```env
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_API_KEY=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_AUTH_DOMAIN=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_PROJECT_ID=
NEXT_PUBLIC_FIREBASE_APP_ID=

FIREBASE_CLIENT_EMAIL=
FIREBASE_PRIVATE_KEY=

AI_PROVIDER=
AI_API_KEY=

REDIS_URL=
REDIS_TOKEN=

PAYMENT_PROVIDER=
PAYMENT_SECRET_KEY=
PAYMENT_WEBHOOK_SECRET=

INTERNAL_API_SECRET=
SENTRY_DSN=
```

Never commit `.env` files or private keys to GitHub.

---

# PART 10 — PHASE-WISE ANTIGRAVITY MASTER PROMPTS

## Global instructions for every phase

Antigravity must:

1. Inspect the existing repository before editing.
2. Preserve stable functionality.
3. Never rewrite working modules without justification.
4. Never invent environment variables or credentials.
5. Use TypeScript strictly.
6. Validate all input with Zod.
7. Enforce server-side authorization.
8. Never expose private user information.
9. Never make financial data client-writable.
10. Write tests for every critical module.
11. Keep a changelog.
12. Run production build after changes.
13. Report completed and incomplete tasks separately.
14. Never claim a test passed unless it actually ran.
15. Stop and report if a required external account, legal approval or credential is missing.

---

## PHASE 0 — Repository Audit & Foundation

**Goal:** Understand the repository and establish a safe development foundation.

```text
You are the Lead Software Architect and Senior
Full-Stack Engineer for JANOSHOKTI.

Your first task is NOT to start coding.

Inspect the complete repository.

Determine:
- Existing Next.js version.
- TypeScript configuration.
- Firebase client configuration.
- Firebase Admin configuration.
- Existing authentication implementation.
- Firestore collections and rules.
- Existing API routes.
- Existing environment variables.
- Deployment configuration.
- Existing dependencies.
- Existing security controls.

Create:

1. ARCHITECTURE.md
2. SRS.md
3. DATABASE_SCHEMA.md
4. API_CONTRACTS.md
5. SECURITY_MODEL.md
6. RBAC_MATRIX.md
7. IMPLEMENTATION_ROADMAP.md
8. ENVIRONMENT_VARIABLES.md
9. RISK_REGISTER.md

Requirements:

- Do not expose secrets.
- Do not modify production functionality.
- Identify security vulnerabilities.
- Identify conflicting dependencies.
- Identify missing tests.
- Identify database migration risks.

Deliver a detailed audit report before making
any architectural changes.

Wait for approval before proceeding.
```

## PHASE 1 — Authentication & Core Foundation

**Goal:** Google authentication, user profile and private data separation.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 1.

Requirements:

1. Firebase Google Authentication.
2. No separate registration form.
3. Automatic user provisioning.
4. Secure user profile.
5. Private profile collection.
6. Profile update.
7. Account suspension support.
8. Account deletion request workflow.
9. Centralized Firebase Admin authentication.
10. Server-side RBAC foundation.

Build:

- Login page.
- Public homepage shell.
- User dashboard.
- Profile page.
- Admin login protection.
- API authentication middleware.

Security:

- Never trust client-provided UID.
- Verify Firebase ID tokens on server.
- Reject suspended users.
- Keep phone numbers private.
- Protect private Firestore documents.
- Never expose Firebase Admin credentials.

Tests:

- Valid login.
- Invalid token.
- Expired token.
- Suspended account.
- Cross-user access.
- Unauthorized admin access.

Run lint, typecheck, tests and production build.

Report all changes.
```

## PHASE 2 — Idea Submission & AI

**Goal:** Complete idea collection and processing.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 2.

Create:

- Idea submission form.
- Idea management dashboard.
- Private submitter information.
- Submission receipt.
- Idea status machine.
- File attachment support.
- Consent management.
- AI processing queue.

AI modules:

1. Language detection.
2. Category classification.
3. Duplicate detection.
4. Summary generation.
5. Feasibility analysis.
6. Budget anomaly detection.
7. Risk identification.
8. Content moderation.

Use a provider-independent AI adapter.

AI must never make final approval decisions.

Requirements:

- Validate all input with Zod.
- Add rate limits.
- Add retry limits.
- Cache unchanged AI results.
- Track AI cost.
- Keep AI logs separate from public content.
- Provide manual fallback.

Create test fixtures for Bangla and English ideas.

Test private data isolation.

Complete the phase only when all tests pass.
```

## PHASE 3 — Public Notice Board & Community

**Goal:** Public idea publication and evaluation.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 3.

Build:

- Public Notice Board.
- Category filtering.
- Location filtering.
- Search.
- Pagination.
- Idea detail pages.
- Anonymous public presentation.
- Five-star rating.
- Constructive comments.
- Positive/negative feedback.
- Report abuse system.
- Moderation dashboard.

Publication:

- Monthly review cycle.
- Last ten days as default publication period.
- Approved ideas only.
- Publication consent required.
- Preserve historical records.

Security:

- No submitter name in public DTO.
- No submitter phone in public DTO.
- No private profile access.
- One active rating per user.
- Prevent self-rating.
- Protect against vote manipulation.

Create automated tests for every permission.
```

## PHASE 4 — Funding & Financial Ledger

**Goal:** Safe donation and accounting infrastructure.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 4.

This is a financial-critical phase.

Do not activate live payments automatically.

First implement a payment sandbox.

Build:

1. General funding campaigns.
2. Project-specific campaigns.
3. Optional donations.
4. Checkout API.
5. Provider adapter.
6. Webhook verification.
7. Idempotency.
8. Donation receipts.
9. Refund records.
10. Double-entry ledger.
11. Reconciliation.
12. Public transparency dashboard.

Rules:

- Store money as integer poisha.
- Never trust browser payment success.
- Never trust client-supplied totals.
- Never modify posted ledger entries.
- Use reversal entries.
- Preserve historical cycles.
- Separate general and restricted project funds.
- Mask donor personal information.
- Enforce dual authorization for high-risk actions.

Require documented legal and merchant approval
before production payment activation.

Test:
- Duplicate webhook.
- Forged webhook.
- Wrong amount.
- Failed payment.
- Refund.
- Partial settlement.
- Ledger imbalance.
- Unauthorized expense approval.

Do not release this phase if any financial
integrity test fails.
```

## PHASE 5 — Project & Transparency Management

**Goal:** Track real project execution.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 5.

Build:

- Project approval workflow.
- Project manager assignment.
- Project budgets.
- Milestones.
- Progress reporting.
- Expense requests.
- Vendor records.
- Expense approval.
- Completion reports.
- Project cancellation.
- Refund and unspent fund handling.
- Impact dashboard.

Every project must have:
- Project ID.
- Approved budget.
- Funding source.
- Responsible manager.
- Timeline.
- Milestones.
- Spending history.
- Public status.

Keep project funding separate from
organization operating funds.

Never allow project managers to approve
their own financial expense requests.

Generate public project reports from
verified records.
```

## PHASE 6 — Governance, HR & Employee Welfare

**Goal:** Add organization-wide management.

```text
Implement JANOSHOKTI Phase 6.

Build governance and HR modules:

- Board management.
- Role assignment.
- Approval workflows.
- Conflict of interest declarations.
- Audit management.
- Employee records.
- Employment contracts.
- Payroll.
- Leave records.
- Welfare accounting.
- Applicable retirement benefits.

Requirements:

- Restrict HR access.
- Restrict salary information.
- Keep employee welfare records separate
  from donation ledgers.
- Enforce segregation of duties.
- Preserve historical changes.
- Add approval history.
- Do not promise unapproved pension benefits.
- Follow applicable Bangladesh employment law.

All sensitive HR APIs must be server-side.
```

## PHASE 7 — Production Hardening

```text
Perform a complete production-readiness audit
for JANOSHOKTI.

Inspect:

- Authentication.
- Authorization.
- Firestore Rules.
- API security.
- Payment security.
- Financial accounting.
- Privacy.
- AI abuse.
- Rate limiting.
- Data retention.
- Backup and restoration.
- Performance.
- Accessibility.
- Mobile responsiveness.
- Monitoring.
- Incident response.

Run:

1. TypeScript checks.
2. ESLint.
3. Unit tests.
4. Integration tests.
5. E2E tests.
6. Firestore Rules Emulator tests.
7. Dependency audit.
8. Production build.
9. Security test suite.

Do not claim production readiness based
on build success alone.

Produce:

- FINAL_AUDIT.md
- SECURITY_REPORT.md
- PERFORMANCE_REPORT.md
- RELEASE_READINESS.md
- REMAINING_RISKS.md

Do not deploy or activate live fundraising
without explicit human approval.
```

---

# FINAL PROJECT ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA

JANOSHOKTI is not considered complete simply because the homepage works.

It must satisfy all of these:

- [ ] Google authentication works.
- [ ] No separate registration form.
- [ ] Private and public data are separated.
- [ ] Ideas can be submitted.
- [ ] AI processing is operational.
- [ ] Human review works.
- [ ] Anonymous public publication works.
- [ ] Ratings and comments work.
- [ ] Monthly cycle works.
- [ ] Donation sandbox works.
- [ ] Financial ledger balances.
- [ ] Duplicate payment events are handled safely.
- [ ] Project milestones work.
- [ ] Public transparency reports work.
- [ ] Admin permissions are tested.
- [ ] Audit logs are protected.
- [ ] Backup restoration is tested.
- [ ] Legal and operational launch requirements are met.
- [ ] Production security review is approved.

**END OF ENGINEERING SPECIFICATION — JANOSHOKTI v1.0**</WritingBlock>

# ১৮. বাস্তবায়ন শুরু করার আগে আমার ৬টি গুরুত্বপূর্ণ Engineering Decision

এগুলো চূড়ান্তভাবে মেনে চলার পরামর্শ দিচ্ছি।

| বিষয় | সিদ্ধান্ত |
|---|---|
| Database | Firestore দিয়ে শুরু |
| Financial Ledger | Double-entry, immutable |
| AI | Provider-independent adapter |
| Payment | প্রথমে Sandbox |
| Android App | একই API ব্যবহার করে পরবর্তীতে |
| Admin Security | RBAC + Dual Approval |

### বিশেষ সতর্কতা: Firestore Rules

উপরের Rules হলো প্রাথমিক নিরাপত্তা কাঠামো, সরাসরি Production-ready দাবি নয়। Antigravity-কে সম্পূর্ণ Schema অনুযায়ী Rules ও Emulator Tests তৈরি করতে হবে। Firebase Admin SDK Rules bypass করে—তাই Backend-এর প্রতিটি privileged operation-এ আলাদা permission validation বাধ্যতামূলক।

### আমার সুপারিশ: কাজ শুরু করার ক্রম

<box border radius="lg" padding={3} gap={2}>
  <row align="center" justify="between">
    **প্রথম কাজ**
    <badge color="success">Start here</badge>
  </row>
  Phase 0-এর Repository Audit Prompt Antigravity-তে দাও।

  <divider color="subtle"/>
  **দ্বিতীয় কাজ**

  Audit Report দেখে Phase 1 অনুমোদন করো।

  <divider color="subtle"/>
  **তৃতীয় কাজ**

  প্রতিটি Phase-এর শেষে Build, Test, Security ও Cost Report নাও।

  <divider color="subtle"/>
  **চতুর্থ কাজ**

  Payment ও HR চালুর আগে প্রয়োজনীয় আইনগত, আর্থিক ও পরিচালনগত অনুমোদন সম্পন্ন করো।
</box>

**একটি নিয়ম কখনো ভাঙবে না:** Antigravity-কে একবারে সাতটি Phase-এর সব কোড লিখতে বলবে না। প্রতিটি Phase-এর কাজ, পরীক্ষা ও অনুমোদন শেষ করে পরবর্তী Phase শুরু করবে।

এতে অপ্রয়োজনীয় কোড, Firebase Read/Write, AI API খরচ এবং ভবিষ্যতের Security Debt অনেকটাই নিয়ন্ত্রণে রাখা যাবে।

আমার মতে, JANOSHOKTI-এর প্রথম বাস্তব লক্ষ্য হওয়া উচিত:

**রংপুরের ১০০টি ভালো আইডিয়া সংগ্রহ, সেগুলোর মধ্যে ২০টি যাচাই এবং অন্তত একটি প্রকল্প বাস্তবে বাস্তবায়নের উপযোগী করে তোলা।**

এটাই হবে তোমার প্ল্যাটফর্মের প্রথম বিশ্বাসযোগ্য সাফল্য।

---

**TARGET AI:** Antigravity 3.8 Flash  
**PROJECT TYPE:** Public Social Innovation & Community Funding Platform  
**INITIAL LOCATION:** Rangpur, Bangladesh  
**FUTURE EXPANSION:** Nationwide Bangladesh  
**FIRST PLATFORM:** Responsive Web Application  
**FUTURE PLATFORM:** Android Application  
**PRIMARY STACK:** Next.js + TypeScript + Firebase + Firestore  
**DEVELOPMENT APPROACH:** Documentation-first, security-first, incremental engineering.

---

# SECTION 0 — YOUR ROLE AND RESPONSIBILITY

You are acting as a complete senior engineering organization.

You must simultaneously perform the responsibilities of:

1. Chief Technology Officer (CTO).
2. Senior Product Manager.
3. Product Architect.
4. Business Analyst.
5. Senior UI/UX Designer.
6. Frontend Architect.
7. Backend Architect.
8. Firebase Architect.
9. AI/ML Integration Engineer.
10. Financial Systems Architect.
11. Cybersecurity Engineer.
12. Database Engineer.
13. DevOps Engineer.
14. QA Automation Engineer.
15. Performance Engineer.
16. Technical Documentation Writer.

Your task is to plan, architect, build, test, secure and prepare JANOSHOKTI for production.

Do not behave like a basic code generator.

Think like an engineering team building a long-term public-interest platform that must handle sensitive personal information, public contributions, financial transactions, public criticism and organizational governance.

**Primary instruction: Correctness, Security, Financial Integrity and Maintainability are more important than development speed.**

# SECTION 1 — PROJECT VISION

JANOSHOKTI is a public social innovation platform.

Its mission is to collect ideas from ordinary people, evaluate them using AI-assisted analysis and human expertise, publish approved ideas anonymously, enable public discussion and voluntary donations, and implement selected projects to create measurable social impact and sustainable employment.

The organization will initially operate in Rangpur, Bangladesh.

It may eventually expand throughout Bangladesh.

## 1.1 Core Business Model

The platform has six major pillars:

1. Public Idea Collection.
2. AI-Powered Idea Classification.
3. Expert Review and Public Evaluation.
4. Voluntary Community Funding.
5. Project Implementation and Transparency.
6. Sustainable Employment and Employee Welfare.

## 1.2 Non-Negotiable Principles

- Idea submission is free.
- Google authentication only.
- No separate password registration system.
- Donations are entirely voluntary.
- No employment fee.
- No promise of guaranteed government-equivalent employment.
- No promise of guaranteed lifelong employment.
- No guaranteed financial return to donors.
- Public donations must be used according to disclosed purposes.
- Personal information must remain private.
- AI must not be the final decision-maker.
- Financial records must never be destructively reset.
- Public financial transparency is mandatory.
- Employment and retirement benefits must follow applicable Bangladesh law.

## 1.3 Branding

Working name: JANOSHOKTI.

The official organization name and domain are not yet finalized.

Design a brand system that allows the name, logo, domain, tagline and institutional identity to be changed without modifying core application logic.

Suggested tagline:

"জনগণের চিন্তা, জনগণের অংশগ্রহণ, জনগণের উন্নয়ন।"

Do not permanently hardcode the working brand name.

# SECTION 7 — AI PROCESSING ENGINE

Create a provider-independent AI architecture.

AI must assist, not replace human governance.

## AI Modules

1. Language Detection.
2. Category Classification.
3. Duplicate Detection.
4. Semantic Similarity.
5. Idea Summarization.
6. Problem Analysis.
7. Feasibility Analysis.
8. Budget Anomaly Detection.
9. Risk Identification.
10. Content Moderation.
11. Missing Information Detection.
12. Reviewer Assistance.

## AI Workflow

Idea Submission
→ Basic Validation
→ Moderation
→ Classification
→ Duplicate Detection
→ Summary Generation
→ Feasibility Analysis
→ Risk Analysis
→ AI Report
→ Human Review Queue.

## AI Requirements

- Background processing.
- Queue-based jobs.
- Retry mechanism.
- Idempotent jobs.
- AI cost tracking.
- Token tracking.
- Provider abstraction.
- Model version tracking.
- Prompt version tracking.
- Cache unchanged results.
- Manual processing fallback.
- AI kill switch.

Never allow AI to independently:

- Approve fundraising.
- Reject an idea permanently.
- Transfer money.
- Appoint employees.
- Decide employment termination.
- Modify financial records.

Create `/docs/ai/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md`.

# SECTION 9 — FUNDRAISING SYSTEM

Two funding types are required.

## A. General Fund

Used only for explicitly disclosed general social activities.

## B. Project-Specific Fund

Restricted to an approved project.

Each campaign requires:

- Campaign ID.
- Campaign purpose.
- Project reference where applicable.
- Target amount.
- Start date.
- End date if applicable.
- Approved budget.
- Funding status.
- Refund and unspent fund policy.

## Donation Features

- Optional donation amount.
- Suggested BDT 100.
- Custom amount.
- Anonymous donation.
- Consented public donor name.
- Receipt generation.
- Payment status.
- Refund tracking.
- Donation history.

Donation must never be connected to employment eligibility or voting rights.

## Payment Architecture

Use an adapter-based payment integration.

Do not hardcode a payment provider.

Payment workflow:

1. Server validates campaign.
2. Server creates pending donation.
3. Server requests provider checkout.
4. User completes payment.
5. Provider webhook arrives.
6. Signature is verified.
7. Provider transaction is independently verified where supported.
8. Idempotency is checked.
9. Financial ledger is posted atomically.
10. Receipt is generated.
11. Public totals are updated.

Never trust a client redirect as proof of payment.

## Initial Release

Only implement sandbox payment processing.

Do not activate real public fundraising until the required legal, banking, merchant and organizational approvals are documented.

---

# SECTION 10 — DOUBLE-ENTRY FINANCIAL LEDGER

This is a critical system.

Implement double-entry accounting.

All money must be represented as integer BDT poisha.

Never use floating-point arithmetic for financial amounts.

Required collections:

- financial_accounts.
- ledger_transactions.
- ledger_entries.
- donations.
- payment_events.
- expenses.
- refunds.
- reconciliation_records.

Every posted transaction must satisfy:

TOTAL DEBITS = TOTAL CREDITS.

Rules:

- Posted ledger entries cannot be edited.
- Corrections use reversal transactions.
- Duplicate events cannot create duplicate ledger postings.
- General funds and restricted project funds must be separated.
- Payment fees must be recorded.
- Refunds must have corresponding accounting entries.
- Daily reconciliation must be supported.
- Financial exports must be available to authorized auditors.

## Public Transparency

Display:

- Total verified donations.
- Total verified expenses.
- Remaining balances.
- Campaign targets.
- Project funding progress.
- Refunds.
- Public reports.

Do not publish private bank credentials, payment authentication data or full sensitive provider references.

## Admin Reset

No destructive financial reset.

Implement:

- Close Funding Cycle.
- Archive Cycle.
- Start New Cycle.
- Reconciliation Check.
- Closure Approval.
- Audit Event.

Old financial records must remain available for authorized inspection.

# SECTION 16 — SECURITY ARCHITECTURE

Create a comprehensive security model.

## Required Controls

- Firestore default-deny rules.
- Firebase ID token verification.
- Centralized RBAC.
- Server-side authorization.
- App Check.
- Rate limiting.
- CSRF protection where applicable.
- Input validation.
- Output sanitization.
- XSS prevention.
- IDOR prevention.
- Secure file uploads.
- MIME validation.
- File size limits.
- Secret management.
- Audit logging.
- Webhook signature verification.
- Payment idempotency.
- Abuse detection.
- Account suspension.
- Backup protection.
- Security monitoring.

## Critical Rules

1. Firebase Admin SDK bypasses Firestore Security Rules. Every privileged operation must perform server-side authorization.
2. Never trust client-supplied UID.
3. Never trust client-supplied financial totals.
4. Never allow client-side financial ledger writes.
5. Never allow users to assign themselves roles.
6. Never expose private submitter details.
7. Never allow administrators to delete financial history.
8. Never expose secret environment variables.
9. Never trust payment redirect status.
10. Never claim production security without testing.

Create:

- `SECURITY_ARCHITECTURE.md`
- `THREAT_MODEL.md`
- `FIRESTORE_SECURITY_RULES.md`
- `INCIDENT_RESPONSE.md`
- `SECURITY_TEST_PLAN.md`

# SECTION 17 — PERFORMANCE AND COST OPTIMIZATION

The application must remain economical at low and medium traffic.

Optimize:

- Firestore reads.
- Firestore writes.
- Repeated API requests.
- AI token consumption.
- Image delivery.
- Client bundle size.
- Server response time.
- Background jobs.
- Unnecessary real-time listeners.

Rules:

- No unrestricted collection scans.
- No loading every user or idea into the browser.
- Use pagination.
- Use server-side filtering.
- Cache public summaries.
- Use lazy-loaded images.
- Use optimized image formats.
- Avoid unnecessary client components.
- Use rate limits.
- Track monthly service costs.

Create:

`/docs/operations/COST_OPTIMIZATION.md`

`/docs/operations/PERFORMANCE_BUDGET.md`

Track actual Firestore reads, writes, storage and AI usage.

Do not enable expensive features without measuring their cost.

- Release-readiness review.

## PHASE 8 — ANDROID PREPARATION

Prepare:
- Stable API contracts.
- Mobile-friendly authentication.
- Push notification architecture.
- Responsive UI.
- API documentation.
- Mobile-specific permission handling.

Do not build an Android app before the web platform and APIs are stable.

# SECTION 21 — DEVELOPMENT EXECUTION RULES

These rules are mandatory.

### RULE 1 — INSPECT BEFORE EDITING

Always inspect existing files and dependencies.

Never assume the repository is empty.

### RULE 2 — DOCUMENT BEFORE IMPLEMENTING

Complete Phase 0 documentation before application implementation.

### RULE 3 — SMALL INCREMENTAL CHANGES

Make logically grouped changes.

Do not rewrite the entire project in one large operation.

### RULE 4 — TEST EVERY MODULE

Every critical module must include automated tests.

### RULE 5 — NO FAKE COMPLETION

Never report:

- Build passed when it did not run.
- Payment verified when it was not verified.
- Security audited when no audit occurred.
- Feature completed when only its UI exists.

### RULE 6 — NO PLACEHOLDER PRODUCTION LOGIC

Do not leave fake payment verification, fake financial records, insecure mock authentication or fabricated public statistics in production code.

### RULE 7 — NO SECRET LEAKAGE

Never print secret keys in logs, reports or generated documentation.

### RULE 8 — COST CONTROL

Avoid unnecessary Firestore reads, writes, AI calls and paid services.

### RULE 9 — PRESERVE STABLE CODE

Before changing existing functionality, understand its dependencies.

### RULE 10 — PHASE APPROVAL

Complete one phase, produce its report and wait for explicit approval before starting the next phase.

# SECTION 0 — YOUR ROLE AND RESPONSIBILITY

You are acting as a complete senior engineering organization.

**Primary instruction: Correctness, Security, Financial Integrity and Maintainability are more important than development speed.**

# SECTION 1 — PROJECT VISION

JANOSHOKTI is a public social innovation platform.

Its mission is to collect ideas from ordinary people, evaluate them using AI-assisted analysis and human expertise, publish approved ideas anonymously, enable public discussion and voluntary donations, and implement selected projects to create measurable social impact and sustainable employment.

The organization will initially operate in Rangpur, Bangladesh.

It may eventually expand throughout Bangladesh.

## 1.1 Core Business Model

The platform has six major pillars:

1. Public Idea Collection.
2. AI-Powered Idea Classification.
3. Expert Review and Public Evaluation.
4. Voluntary Community Funding.
5. Project Implementation and Transparency.
6. Sustainable Employment and Employee Welfare.

## 1.2 Non-Negotiable Principles

- Idea submission is free.
- Google authentication only.
- No separate password registration system.
- Donations are entirely voluntary.
- No employment fee.
- No promise of guaranteed government-equivalent employment.
- No promise of guaranteed lifelong employment.
- No guaranteed financial return to donors.
- Public donations must be used according to disclosed purposes.
- Personal information must remain private.
- AI must not be the final decision-maker.
- Financial records must never be destructively reset.
- Public financial transparency is mandatory.
- Employment and retirement benefits must follow applicable Bangladesh law.

## 1.3 Branding

Working name: JANOSHOKTI.

The official organization name and domain are not yet finalized.

Design a brand system that allows the name, logo, domain, tagline and institutional identity to be changed without modifying core application logic.

Suggested tagline:

"জনগণের চিন্তা, জনগণের অংশগ্রহণ, জনগণের উন্নয়ন।"

Do not permanently hardcode the working brand name.

# SECTION 7 — AI PROCESSING ENGINE

Create a provider-independent AI architecture.

AI must assist, not replace human governance.

## AI Modules

1. Language Detection.
2. Category Classification.
3. Duplicate Detection.
4. Semantic Similarity.
5. Idea Summarization.
6. Problem Analysis.
7. Feasibility Analysis.
8. Budget Anomaly Detection.
9. Risk Identification.
10. Content Moderation.
11. Missing Information Detection.
12. Reviewer Assistance.

## AI Workflow

Idea Submission
→ Basic Validation
→ Moderation
→ Classification
→ Duplicate Detection
→ Summary Generation
→ Feasibility Analysis
→ Risk Analysis
→ AI Report
→ Human Review Queue.

## AI Requirements

- Background processing.
- Queue-based jobs.
- Retry mechanism.
- Idempotent jobs.
- AI cost tracking.
- Token tracking.
- Provider abstraction.
- Model version tracking.
- Prompt version tracking.
- Cache unchanged results.
- Manual processing fallback.
- AI kill switch.

Never allow AI to independently:

- Approve fundraising.
- Reject an idea permanently.
- Transfer money.
- Appoint employees.
- Decide employment termination.
- Modify financial records.

Create `/docs/ai/AI_ARCHITECTURE.md`.

# SECTION 9 — FUNDRAISING SYSTEM

Two funding types are required.

## A. General Fund

Used only for explicitly disclosed general social activities.

## B. Project-Specific Fund

Restricted to an approved project.

Each campaign requires:

- Campaign ID.
- Campaign purpose.
- Project reference where applicable.
- Target amount.
- Start date.
- End date if applicable.
- Approved budget.
- Funding status.
- Refund and unspent fund policy.

## Donation Features

- Optional donation amount.
- Suggested BDT 100.
- Custom amount.
- Anonymous donation.
- Consented public donor name.
- Receipt generation.
- Payment status.
- Refund tracking.
- Donation history.

Donation must never be connected to employment eligibility or voting rights.

## Payment Architecture

Use an adapter-based payment integration.

Do not hardcode a payment provider.

Payment workflow:

1. Server validates campaign.
2. Server creates pending donation.
3. Server requests provider checkout.
4. User completes payment.
5. Provider webhook arrives.
6. Signature is verified.
7. Provider transaction is independently verified where supported.
8. Idempotency is checked.
9. Financial ledger is posted atomically.
10. Receipt is generated.
11. Public totals are updated.

Never trust a client redirect as proof of payment.

## Initial Release

Only implement sandbox payment processing.

Do not activate real public fundraising until the required legal, banking, merchant and organizational approvals are documented.

---

# SECTION 10 — DOUBLE-ENTRY FINANCIAL LEDGER

This is a critical system.

Implement double-entry accounting.

All money must be represented as integer BDT poisha.

Never use floating-point arithmetic for financial amounts.

Required collections:

- financial_accounts.
- ledger_transactions.
- ledger_entries.
- donations.
- payment_events.
- expenses.
- refunds.
- reconciliation_records.

Every posted transaction must satisfy:

TOTAL DEBITS = TOTAL CREDITS.

Rules:

- Posted ledger entries cannot be edited.
- Corrections use reversal transactions.
- Duplicate events cannot create duplicate ledger postings.
- General funds and restricted project funds must be separated.
- Payment fees must be recorded.
- Refunds must have corresponding accounting entries.
- Daily reconciliation must be supported.
- Financial exports must be available to authorized auditors.

## Public Transparency

Display:

- Total verified donations.
- Total verified expenses.
- Remaining balances.
- Campaign targets.
- Project funding progress.
- Refunds.
- Public reports.

Do not publish private bank credentials, payment authentication data or full sensitive provider references.

## Admin Reset

No destructive financial reset.

Implement:

- Close Funding Cycle.
- Archive Cycle.
- Start New Cycle.
- Reconciliation Check.
- Closure Approval.
- Audit Event.

Old financial records must remain available for authorized inspection.

---

- GET /auth/me
- POST /auth/account-deletion-request

Ideas:
- POST /ideas
- GET /ideas
- GET /ideas/:id
- GET /ideas/mine
- PATCH /ideas/:id
- POST /ideas/:id/withdraw

Reviews:
- GET /admin/ideas/queue
- POST /admin/ideas/:id/reviews
- POST /admin/ideas/:id/decision
- POST /admin/ideas/:id/publish

Community:
- PUT /ideas/:id/rating
- POST /ideas/:id/comments
- GET /ideas/:id/comments
- POST /comments/:id/report

AI:
- POST /internal/ai/jobs
- GET /admin/ai/jobs
- POST /admin/ai/jobs/:id/retry

Projects:
- GET /projects
- GET /projects/:id
- POST /admin/projects
- PATCH /admin/projects/:id
- POST /admin/projects/:id/milestones

Funding:
- GET /funding/campaigns
- POST /donations/checkout
- GET /donations/:id/status
- GET /donations/receipts/:receiptId
- POST /payments/webhooks/:provider

Finance:
- GET /admin/finance/overview
- GET /admin/finance/ledger
- POST /admin/expenses
- POST /admin/expenses/:id/approve
- POST /admin/refunds

Transparency:
- GET /transparency/summary
- GET /transparency/reports

Governance:
- GET /admin/audit-logs
- GET /admin/roles
- POST /admin/roles/assign
- POST /admin/cycles
- POST /admin/cycles/:id/close

For every API specify:

- HTTP method.
- Authentication requirement.
- Permission requirement.
- Request schema.
- Response schema.
- Error codes.
- Rate limits.
- Idempotency rules.
- Audit requirements.

Use Zod for request and response validation.

Generate OpenAPI documentation.

---

# SECTION 16 — SECURITY ARCHITECTURE

Create a comprehensive security model.

## Required Controls

- Firestore default-deny rules.
- Firebase ID token verification.
- Centralized RBAC.
- Server-side authorization.
- App Check.
- Rate limiting.
- CSRF protection where applicable.
- Input validation.
- Output sanitization.
- XSS prevention.
- IDOR prevention.
- Secure file uploads.
- MIME validation.
- File size limits.
- Secret management.
- Audit logging.
- Webhook signature verification.
- Payment idempotency.
- Abuse detection.
- Account suspension.
- Backup protection.
- Security monitoring.

## Critical Rules

1. Firebase Admin SDK bypasses Firestore Security Rules. Every privileged operation must perform server-side authorization.
2. Never trust client-supplied UID.
3. Never trust client-supplied financial totals.
4. Never allow client-side financial ledger writes.
5. Never allow users to assign themselves roles.
6. Never expose private submitter details.
7. Never allow administrators to delete financial history.
8. Never expose secret environment variables.
9. Never trust payment redirect status.
10. Never claim production security without testing.

Create:

- `SECURITY_ARCHITECTURE.md`
- `THREAT_MODEL.md`
- `FIRESTORE_SECURITY_RULES.md`
- `INCIDENT_RESPONSE.md`
- `SECURITY_TEST_PLAN.md`

---

# SECTION 17 — PERFORMANCE AND COST OPTIMIZATION

The application must remain economical at low and medium traffic.

Optimize:

- Firestore reads.
- Firestore writes.
- Repeated API requests.
- AI token consumption.
- Image delivery.
- Client bundle size.
- Server response time.
- Background jobs.
- Unnecessary real-time listeners.

Rules:

- No unrestricted collection scans.
- No loading every user or idea into the browser.
- Use pagination.
- Use server-side filtering.
- Cache public summaries.
- Use lazy-loaded images.
- Use optimized image formats.
- Avoid unnecessary client components.
- Use rate limits.
- Track monthly service costs.

Create:

`/docs/operations/COST_OPTIMIZATION.md`

`/docs/operations/PERFORMANCE_BUDGET.md`

Track actual Firestore reads, writes, storage and AI usage.

Do not enable expensive features without measuring their cost.

## Integration Tests

## Security Tests

- Unauthorized expense approval.
- Admin impersonation.

Complete user journey:

Google Sign-in
→ Idea Submission
→ AI Processing
→ Human Review
→ Publication
→ Public Rating
→ Comment
→ Donation Sandbox
→ Receipt
→ Project Progress
→ Transparency Report.

Use Firebase Emulator Suite.

Use payment sandbox credentials.

Never use real donor funds in automated tests.

# SECTION 19 — DEPLOYMENT ARCHITECTURE

Environments:

Development
→ Staging
→ Production.

Each environment must have separate:

- Firebase project.
- Firestore database.
- Storage.
- API credentials.
- Payment sandbox/production configuration.
- Environment variables.
- Monitoring configuration.

Use:

- GitHub.
- Vercel.
- Cloudflare.
- Firebase.
- Sentry.

Deployment requirements:

- Automated build.
- Automated tests.
- Environment validation.
- Database index deployment.
- Security rules deployment.
- Rollback strategy.
- Backup verification.
- Monitoring.
- Incident alerts.

Never deploy a financial-critical change directly to production without staging tests.

# SECTION 20 — PHASE-WISE IMPLEMENTATION STRATEGY

Do not build the entire platform in one operation.

Implement in this order:

## PHASE 0 — AUDIT AND DOCUMENTATION

Deliver:
- Complete repository audit.
- PRD.
- SRS.
- Architecture.
- Database schema.
- API contracts.
- Threat model.
- Roadmap.
- Risk register.

No production code changes without approval.

## PHASE 1 — CORE FOUNDATION

Implement:

- Google authentication.
- User profiles.
- Private profiles.
- RBAC.
- Public homepage.
- Dashboard.
- Basic admin dashboard.
- Firestore rules.

## PHASE 2 — IDEA MANAGEMENT

Implement:
- Idea submission.
- Drafts.
- Private identity storage.
- Idea status machine.
- AI classification.
- Duplicate detection.
- AI reports.
- Human review.

## PHASE 3 — PUBLIC COMMUNITY

Implement:
- Notice board.
- Category filtering.
- Anonymous publication.
- Star rating.
- Comments.
- Reports.
- Moderation.

## PHASE 4 — FUNDRAISING SANDBOX

Implement:
- General fund.
- Project funds.
- Donation checkout sandbox.
- Webhook verification.
- Double-entry ledger.
- Receipts.
- Reconciliation.
- Transparency dashboard.

No live fundraising activation without approval.

## PHASE 5 — PROJECT IMPLEMENTATION

Implement:
- Project dashboard.
- Budgets.
- Milestones.
- Expenses.
- Vendor records.
- Progress reports.
- Impact reports.

## PHASE 6 — GOVERNANCE AND HR

Implement:
- Governance.
- Board workflows.
- Conflict-of-interest controls.
- Employee records.
- Payroll.
- Welfare accounting.

## PHASE 7 — PRODUCTION HARDENING

Perform:
- Full security audit.
- Performance audit.
- Cost audit.
- Privacy audit.
- Disaster recovery test.
- Penetration testing.
- Release-readiness review.

## PHASE 8 — ANDROID PREPARATION

Prepare:
- Stable API contracts.
- Mobile-friendly authentication.
- Push notification architecture.
- Responsive UI.
- API documentation.
- Mobile-specific permission handling.

Do not build an Android app before the web platform and APIs are stable.

# SECTION 21 — DEVELOPMENT EXECUTION RULES

These rules are mandatory.

### RULE 1 — INSPECT BEFORE EDITING

Always inspect existing files and dependencies.

Never assume the repository is empty.

### RULE 2 — DOCUMENT BEFORE IMPLEMENTING

Complete Phase 0 documentation before application implementation.

### RULE 3 — SMALL INCREMENTAL CHANGES

Make logically grouped changes.

Do not rewrite the entire project in one large operation.

### RULE 4 — TEST EVERY MODULE

Every critical module must include automated tests.

### RULE 5 — NO FAKE COMPLETION

Never report:

- Build passed when it did not run.
- Payment verified when it was not verified.
- Security audited when no audit occurred.
- Feature completed when only its UI exists.

### RULE 6 — NO PLACEHOLDER PRODUCTION LOGIC

Do not leave fake payment verification, fake financial records, insecure mock authentication or fabricated public statistics in production code.

### RULE 7 — NO SECRET LEAKAGE

Never print secret keys in logs, reports or generated documentation.

### RULE 8 — COST CONTROL

Avoid unnecessary Firestore reads, writes, AI calls and paid services.

### RULE 9 — PRESERVE STABLE CODE

Before changing existing functionality, understand its dependencies.

### RULE 10 — PHASE APPROVAL

Complete one phase, produce its report and wait for explicit approval before starting the next phase.

# SECTION 23 — FINAL INSTRUCTIONS TO ANTIGRAVITY

Your first response must be a structured project assessment.

Do not immediately generate the complete application.

First:

1. Inspect the repository.
2. Determine existing implementation status.
3. Identify architectural conflicts.
4. Identify security issues.
5. Produce the complete PRD.

9. Produce API contracts.
10. Produce the implementation roadmap.

Then report:

- What should be preserved.
- What needs correction.
- What is risky.
- What must be decided.
- What is ready for implementation.

After documentation, stop and request approval for Phase 1.

Do not assume missing legal approvals, payment credentials, organization registration or operational permissions.

Do not invent credentials, external accounts, user identities or financial records.

Do not activate real payments automatically.

Do not deploy to production without explicit approval.

## DEFINITION OF SUCCESS

JANOSHOKTI will be successful when:

- People can submit ideas freely.
- Their private identities remain protected.
- AI organizes ideas efficiently.
- Qualified humans evaluate proposals.
- The public can provide constructive feedback.
- Donations are voluntary and traceable.
- Every financial transaction is accountable.
- Projects publish genuine progress.
- Social impact can be independently evaluated.
- Employment and employee welfare are managed responsibly.

**Build an institution-grade platform, not just a visually attractive website.**

Start with Phase 0: Complete Repository Audit and Product Documentation.

Wait for approval before Phase 1.

2. JANOSHOKTI-এর জন্য একটি নতুন GitHub Repository অথবা নিরাপদ Project Folder খোলো।

6. প্রতিটি Phase শেষে Git Commit তৈরি করো।

**আমার বিশেষ পরামর্শ:** Antigravity-কে একবারে পুরো সফটওয়্যার বানাতে দেবে না। Flash মডেলের জন্য ছোট, নির্দিষ্ট এবং পরীক্ষাযোগ্য কাজ ভাগ করে দেওয়া বেশি নিয়ন্ত্রণযোগ্য।

প্রথমে `PRD.md`, `SYSTEM_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `FIRESTORE_SCHEMA.md` এবং `SECURITY_ARCHITECTURE.md` তৈরি ও পর্যালোচনা করো। এগুলোই পরবর্তী সব Development-এর ভিত্তি হবে।

### 👤 User

শুনো শুধু রংপুর উল্লেখ করলে অন্য জেলার মানুষের ভরসা বা ফান্ড পাওয়া কঠিন, সো এমন করো ফ্রি তে সবাই আইডিয়া সেন্ড করতে পারবে, নোটিস বোর্ডে যে আইডিয়া গুলোর র‍্যাংক ১ম দিয়ে থাকবে, পজেটিভ নেগেটিভ আইডিয়া আপগ্রেডের মাধ্যমে সেগুলো থেকে যেকোনো একটা বাছাই করা হবে আর যে লোকেশনে আইডিয়ার বিজ সেখানেই প্রথম প্রজেক্ট। আর প্রজেক্টে টাকা দেওয়ারাই ওইখানে চাকরির এবং পরবর্তীতে সারা দেশের সকল ব্রাঞ্চের পদ গুলোতে অগ্রাধিকার পাবে। আর পদে আবেদনের সময় টাকার সেন্ড ডকুমেন্ট দেখাতে হবে প্রজেক্ট অনুযায়ী যা ভিন্ন হবে। আর তুমি এন্টিগ্রাভিটি এর ফ্লাস এর জন্য আলাদা আলাদা করতে বললা নির্দেশ তাই তুমিই আলাদা আলাদা করেই প্রমট দেও তাইলে যাতে ওয় গুলায় না ফেলে সব। কোন প্রমট কতো নাম্বারে দিবো সেটা তোমাকেই দিতে হবে। আর গিঠাব রিপো তোমাকে শেয়ার করবো দেখবা সব ঠিক ঠাক আছে কি না?

---

### 🤖 Assistant

তোমার নতুন পরিকল্পনায় একটি গুরুত্বপূর্ণ পরিবর্তন এসেছে। এখন JANOSHOKTI শুধু রংপুরকেন্দ্রিক উদ্যোগ থাকবে না; এটি হবে **সারা বাংলাদেশের মানুষের জন্য উন্মুক্ত একটি জাতীয় সামাজিক উদ্ভাবন প্ল্যাটফর্ম।**

তোমার কথার সঙ্গে আমি একমত যে, প্রথম থেকেই শুধু রংপুরের নাম সামনে রাখলে অন্য জেলার মানুষের কাছে উদ্যোগটি স্থানীয় প্রকল্প বলে মনে হতে পারে।

আমরা নতুনভাবে তিনটি বিষয় নির্ধারণ করব:

1. আইডিয়া জমা দিতে পারবে বাংলাদেশের যেকোনো জেলার মানুষ।
2. যে আইডিয়া নির্বাচিত হবে, প্রথম প্রকল্প বাস্তবায়নের চেষ্টা হবে সেই আইডিয়ার প্রস্তাবিত লোকেশনেই।
3. ভবিষ্যতে বিভিন্ন জেলায় প্রকল্প ও শাখা তৈরি হবে।

তবে চাকরির বিষয়ে একটি গুরুত্বপূর্ণ সংশোধন প্রস্তাব করছি।

**অনুদানদাতাকে চাকরিতে অগ্রাধিকার দেওয়া যাবে না।** এতে আর্থিক সামর্থ্যের ভিত্তিতে চাকরির সুযোগ তৈরি হবে এবং অনুদান কার্যত চাকরির প্রবেশমূল্যে পরিণত হতে পারে। এটি মানুষের আস্থা ও প্রতিষ্ঠানের ন্যায্য নিয়োগনীতি—দুটোরই ক্ষতি করতে পারে।

এর পরিবর্তে:
- অনুদানদাতারা প্রকল্পের অগ্রগতি ও চাকরির বিজ্ঞপ্তির নোটিফিকেশন পেতে পারেন।
- প্রকল্পে স্থানীয়ভাবে নিয়োগের ক্ষেত্রে ওই এলাকার যোগ্য প্রার্থীরা আবেদন করতে পারবেন।
- সব চাকরিতে যোগ্যতা, দক্ষতা ও স্বচ্ছ মূল্যায়নের ভিত্তিতে নিয়োগ হবে।
- চাকরির আবেদনে অনুদানের Transaction ID বা পেমেন্ট ডকুমেন্ট লাগবে না।
- প্রকল্পের জন্য অর্থ দেওয়া এবং চাকরি পাওয়া সম্পূর্ণ পৃথক থাকবে।

এতে তোমার প্রতিষ্ঠানের বিশ্বাসযোগ্যতা অনেক বেশি বাড়বে।

## নতুন Ranking System

শুধু বেশি স্টার পেলেই আইডিয়া বিজয়ী হবে না। প্রাথমিকভাবে এই স্কোরিং মডেল প্রস্তাব করছি:

| মূল্যায়ন | ওজন |
|---|---:|
| Public Rating | ২৫% |
| Positive/Negative Feedback Analysis | ১৫% |
| Expert Review | ২৫% |
| Feasibility | ২০% |
| Social Impact | ১৫% |
| **মোট** | **১০০%** |

এটি প্রস্তাবিত স্কোরিং। AI-এর বিশ্লেষণ হবে সহায়ক; চূড়ান্ত স্কোর ও নির্বাচন হবে যাচাইযোগ্য মানবিক মূল্যায়নের মাধ্যমে।

শীর্ষ আইডিয়াগুলো Idea Improvement পর্যায়ে যাবে। প্রস্তাবক চাইলে পরামর্শ অনুযায়ী সংশোধিত সংস্করণ জমা দেবেন। এরপর বোর্ড নির্বাচনের কারণ প্রকাশ করবে।

---

# ২. Antigravity 3.8 Flash-এর জন্য আলাদা আলাদা Prompt

আমি মোট ৯টি Prompt সাজাচ্ছি। প্রতিটি আলাদা করে কপি করবে। একসঙ্গে সব পেস্ট করবে না।

| Prompt | কাজ | কখন দেবে |
|---|---|---|
| 00 | Repository Audit | প্রথমে |
| 01 | PRD + SRS + Architecture | Audit শেষে |
| 02 | UI/UX + Design System | Documentation শেষে |
| 03 | Authentication + Database | Phase 1 |
| 04 | Idea + AI + Ranking | Phase 2 |
| 05 | Project Management | Phase 3 |
| 06 | Funding + Ledger | Phase 4 |
| 07 | Governance + HR | Phase 5 |
| 08 | Security + Production Audit | সবশেষে |

**গুরুত্বপূর্ণ:** প্রতিটি Prompt-এর শেষে Stop & Report নির্দেশ থাকবে। কাজ শেষ না করে পরের Prompt-এ যাবে না।



---

### 🤖 Assistant

তোমার নতুন পরিকল্পনায় একটি গুরুত্বপূর্ণ পরিবর্তন এসেছে। এখন JANOSHOKTI শুধু রংপুরকেন্দ্রিক উদ্যোগ থাকবে না; এটি হবে **সারা বাংলাদেশের মানুষের জন্য উন্মুক্ত একটি জাতীয় সামাজিক উদ্ভাবন প্ল্যাটফর্ম।**

তোমার কথার সঙ্গে আমি একমত যে, প্রথম থেকেই শুধু রংপুরের নাম সামনে রাখলে অন্য জেলার মানুষের কাছে উদ্যোগটি স্থানীয় প্রকল্প বলে মনে হতে পারে।

আমরা নতুনভাবে তিনটি বিষয় নির্ধারণ করব:

1. আইডিয়া জমা দিতে পারবে বাংলাদেশের যেকোনো জেলার মানুষ।
2. যে আইডিয়া নির্বাচিত হবে, প্রথম প্রকল্প বাস্তবায়নের চেষ্টা হবে সেই আইডিয়ার প্রস্তাবিত লোকেশনেই।
3. ভবিষ্যতে বিভিন্ন জেলায় প্রকল্প ও শাখা তৈরি হবে।

তবে চাকরির বিষয়ে একটি গুরুত্বপূর্ণ সংশোধন প্রস্তাব করছি।

**অনুদানদাতাকে চাকরিতে অগ্রাধিকার দেওয়া যাবে না।** এতে আর্থিক সামর্থ্যের ভিত্তিতে চাকরির সুযোগ তৈরি হবে এবং অনুদান কার্যত চাকরির প্রবেশমূল্যে পরিণত হতে পারে। এটি মানুষের আস্থা ও প্রতিষ্ঠানের ন্যায্য নিয়োগনীতি—দুটোরই ক্ষতি করতে পারে।

এর পরিবর্তে:
- অনুদানদাতারা প্রকল্পের অগ্রগতি ও চাকরির বিজ্ঞপ্তির নোটিফিকেশন পেতে পারেন।
- প্রকল্পে স্থানীয়ভাবে নিয়োগের ক্ষেত্রে ওই এলাকার যোগ্য প্রার্থীরা আবেদন করতে পারবেন।
- সব চাকরিতে যোগ্যতা, দক্ষতা ও স্বচ্ছ মূল্যায়নের ভিত্তিতে নিয়োগ হবে।
- চাকরির আবেদনে অনুদানের Transaction ID বা পেমেন্ট ডকুমেন্ট লাগবে না।
- প্রকল্পের জন্য অর্থ দেওয়া এবং চাকরি পাওয়া সম্পূর্ণ পৃথক থাকবে।

এতে তোমার প্রতিষ্ঠানের বিশ্বাসযোগ্যতা অনেক বেশি বাড়বে।

## নতুন Ranking System

শুধু বেশি স্টার পেলেই আইডিয়া বিজয়ী হবে না। প্রাথমিকভাবে এই স্কোরিং মডেল প্রস্তাব করছি:

| মূল্যায়ন | ওজন |
|---|---:|
| Public Rating | ২৫% |
| Positive/Negative Feedback Analysis | ১৫% |
| Expert Review | ২৫% |
| Feasibility | ২০% |
| Social Impact | ১৫% |
| **মোট** | **১০০%** |

এটি প্রস্তাবিত স্কোরিং। AI-এর বিশ্লেষণ হবে সহায়ক; চূড়ান্ত স্কোর ও নির্বাচন হবে যাচাইযোগ্য মানবিক মূল্যায়নের মাধ্যমে।

শীর্ষ আইডিয়াগুলো Idea Improvement পর্যায়ে যাবে। প্রস্তাবক চাইলে পরামর্শ অনুযায়ী সংশোধিত সংস্করণ জমা দেবেন। এরপর বোর্ড নির্বাচনের কারণ প্রকাশ করবে।
